Upstream tech – AI Semi & Supply Chain  
U.S. CSP capex to be +85%/+53%/+20% in 2026/2027/2028 
Given capex expands further into 2027, CSPs free cash flow are undergoing significant pressure and 
turning negative except Microsoft, such that shifting to debt financing for their massive capex. In 2025, 
Top 5 CSPs’ total debt issuance reached $108bn, or 24% of total capex . Per our Addressing 2028 
Capex and AI Platform Value Shift report on Aug 6, we forecast Top 5 CSPs’ debt issuance to reach 
$257bn/$419bn in 2026/2027, or 31%/35% of total capex.  Assuming debt issuance to grow in 
2028/2029 at a mild pace, we believe U.S. CSPs' balance sheet (except Oracle) to remain very healthy, 
due to net leverage (EBITDA basis) of only 0.5 and the net debt-to-equity ratio of only 18% by 2029.  
Despite healthy balance sheet for most CSPs, we expect most to safeguard balance sheets beyond 
2027 amid widening spreads and FCF profiles, with the focus shifting to returns. Together with a swing 
factor of competition from Chinese LLMs, we expect U.S. CSPs to decelerate capex expansion. We 
forecast Top 5 CSPs capex of $1,512bn/1,739bn in 2028/2029, or +20%/15% YoY, down from +53% 
in 2027.

Server CPU TAM to grow at 49% CAGR by 2030 
As the orchestration core of AI infrastructure buildouts, the Server CPU has gained significant traction 
over the past few months. This is driven by the Agentic AI/inference trend, fueled by the breakthrough 
of OpenClaw and Anthropic’s ARR surging to $44bn  in April. For instance, in latest earnings calls, 
AMD projected a >35% CAGR for Server CPU TAM by 2030, and Intel said the shift toward agentic 
AI is driving a structural demand for CPU, where the GPU-to-CPU ratio is tightening from 8:1 in training 
to 4:1 in inference. Based on our estimates, assuming inference accounts for 90% of AI workloads 
and GPU-to-CPU ratio for AI servers to reach 1:1.2 in 2030, we now expect Server CPU TAM to grow 
96%/71% in 2026/2027, reaching $215bn by 2030 from $30bn in 2025, with a 5-yr CAGR of 49%.

CoWoS tracker: fine tuning upwardly for total capacity 
Following our earlier raise in May Edition on May 12 in which we revised up TSMC’s CoWoS capacity 
from 175KPM to 195KPM by end-2027, the Street has moved up to our numbers , and we have fine 
tune our estimate to 200KPM by end-2027 on our Sept Edition on Sept 14.

2026/2027/2028, and ASE’s CoWoS capacity of 15KPM/45KPM/55KPM by end 2026/2027/2028.  
For fabless, we keep our CoWoS estimates largely unchanged for AI GPU/ASIC. For our accelerator 
model, we maintain +98% YoY 2027E, and for 2028E, we now expect it to reach the TAM of $1,000bn.  
In our ASIC Update report on Oct 13, we lowered our TPU units in 2028 to 10m, vs. 8.9m in 2027.

Upstream tech – Optical 
Strong demand for pluggable through 2028. 
We expect that the total demand for 800G/1.6T will reach 90mn/80mn in 2027, increased by 
80%/167%+, mainly driven by: 1) Nvidia, Google and AWS and other ASIC’s Accelerators’ demand 
are ramping up; 2) GPU/ASIC scale -out bandwidth continuously  increase drives increasing 
GPU/ASIC-to-optical module ratio; 3) We expect that TPU for 2027 will be 8.9m and Google fully 
transitions to large -scale clusters in 2026 and the scale -up layer will adopt optical interconnects. In 
addition, as we expect that CPO/NPO will start since 2H27, represents a pure incremental opportunity 
for optical interconnects supply chain, benefiting FAU, laser, shuffle box, connector and OE players, 
including LITE, GLW, TSEM, Sumitomo and Browave. We also like Marvell and SMTC, for the 
TIA/Driver used in NPO OE.  
Scale-out bandwidth increase driving optical-module deployment 
For NVIDIA, each Rubin GPU is equipped with two CX9 NIC chips, doubling the scale-out bandwidth 
compared with Blackwell. We further estimate that Rubin Ultra will adopt four CX9 chips per GPU, 
leading to another 2 times increase in scale-out bandwidth, continuing NVIDIA’s consistent bandwidth-
doubling trajectory. Accordingly, we expect the ratio of 1.6T optical modules has risen from 1:2.5 to 
1:5 in Rubin. From CoWoS perspective, we expect 5m/2m Blackwell/Rubin and 20m 1.6T in 2026. 
For Google, we believe that Goo gle fully transition to large -scale clusters in 2026 and the scale -up 
layer will adopt optical interconnects. We believe that in the scale-up domain, the ratio of TPU to 1.6T 
optical modules is approximately 1:1.5; In the scale-out domain, the ratio is around 1:2.5, suggesting 
the overall TPU-to-optics ratio is approximately 1:4 (1.6T equivalent).

Nvidia/CSPs’ Scale-out/up CPO/NPO  
Within NVIDIA’s next -gen NVL576 and broader CSPs’ AI infrastructure plans, CPO and NPO are 
developed side -by-side to expand GP U/ASIC interconnect bandwidth: CPO is the ideal long -term 
design for lower power and latency yet faces headwinds from complicated advanced packaging, and 
yield risks, while NPO could deliver most short -reach optical performance gains with far better 
manufacturability and deployment flexibility, making NPO a reliable near -term scaling path and CPO 
the ultimate end -state architecture. NPO partially removes module -side DSP and shifts signal 
conditioning and equalization to system designs, greatly lifting the value of TIAs and laser drivers that 
take over part of traditional DSP functions; we estimate each 3.2T optical engine contains tens of 
dollars worth of combined TIA and driver chips, a clear improvement over standard pluggable optics. 
Our shipment forecasts at 6.5m/42.4m NPO optical engines in CY27/28.  
Scale-up domain to increase by NVL576 to compensate lower memory: With lower memory spec, we 
expect Nvidia to increase the scale -up domain by optics to enhance performance. According to 
Nvidia’s GTC 2026, th e optical scale -up fabric allows Nvidia to expand Rubin Ultra to an NVL576 
scale-up. Regarding CPO and NPO which are being parallelly developed, we expect NPO to be a 
high reliability scaling path for NPO. We now expect its scale -up to adopt NPO in Rubin U ltra and 
Feynman platform and estimate OE shipments of Nvidia’s platform to be 6.0m/19.7m in 2027/2028.

### Page 25

Sector Report | Technology

Nvidia’s NPO OE to be 6.0m/19.7m in 2027/2028 
For Nvidia, we now expect its scale up to adopt NPO in Rubin Ultra and Feynman platform and 
estimate OE  shipments of Nvidia’s platform to be 6.0m/19.7m in 2027/2028, based on full NPO 
adoption at NVL576 launch given NPO maturity. Coupled with other CSP’s platform, we estimate total 
OE market to be 6.5m/42.4m in 2027/2028.  
TIA/Driver to benefit from NPO 
NPO partially removes module -side DSP and shifts signal conditioning and equalization to system 
designs, greatly lifting the value of TIAs and laser drivers that take over part of traditional DSP 
functions; we estimate each 3.2T optical engine contains tens o f dollars worth of combined TIA and 
driver chips, a clear improvement over standard pluggable optics.  
A key architectural shift under NPO is the partial removal of DSP functionality at the module level, with 
signal conditioning and equalization increasing ly redistributed into system -level design. This 
significantly elevates the importance of TIA (transimpedance amplifier) and laser driver components, 
which effectively replace a portion of traditional DSP value. We estimate that each 3.2T optical engine 
will embed high tens of dollars of combined TIA and driver content, representing a meaningful step -
up versus traditional pluggable optics architectures.  
From a competitive standpoint, we expect Marvell to remain the primary beneficiary of both NPO OE 
given i ts stronger positioning high speed TIA/Driver. Semtech is well positioned as a secondary 
beneficiary through its exposure to high-speed analog signal chain components, particularly TIAs and 
laser drivers, which scale directly with optical engine proliferat ion under NPO. We forecast 
6.5mn/42.4mn NPO OE shipments in CY27/28. Based on our estimated front-end content per OE and 
a 30% market share assumption for Semtech, we estimate its NPO revenue of $315/936mn. 
Stock Implication 
Generally speaking, scale-up CPO/NPO is an incremental opportunity for optical interconnect supply 
chain, as it aims to replace copper and has no impact on scale -out side. Additionally, scale up and 
scale out CPO solution share the similar suppliers. That said, we expect the key benefic iaries to be 
components such as FAU, CW laser, ELSFP, shuffle, PIC, TIA and Driver. We: 1) prefer Lumentum/ 
COHR due to the demand upside for CW laser, and also like local cw laser players; 2) expect Browave 
(3163 TT) to benefit, given the sizable contribu tion of CPO’s shuffle box considering ~55% market 
share, $5-6k ASP and a volume ramp in 2H26; 3) we also like TSEM, given CSP preferring NPO 
solution for their ASICs scale up interconnection. 4) expect GLW to benefit, given the second supplier 
of shuffle b ox and main supplier of MMC connector/fiber. 5) Marvell/Semtech will also benefit from 
CSP/Nvidia’s NPO solution, since TIA and driver will upgrade in the NPO Optical Engine.

### Page 28

Sector Report | Technology

Google’s 6D Torus & Beneficiaries.  
We expect Google will migrate its TPU interconnect from 3D to 6D torus, re -organizing the 64 -chip 
elemental block from 4× 4× 4 to 2× 2× 2× 2× 2× 2. External optical links per block rise from 96 to 384, with 
optical ports per chip scaling increase from 1:1.5 to 1:6, driving 4x coherent lite transceivers and OCS 
demand. The trajectory aligns with NVIDIA's CPO/NPO move. Both are interconnect scaling that 
expands direct chip-to-chip bandwidth and reduces hop count, with TPU via a 6D torus and NVIDIA 
via CPO/NPO for Scale-up. 
Google’s migration from 3D  to 6D Torus: We expect Google to migrate its TPU interconnect from a 
3D to a 6D torus topology, re-organizing the elemental 64-chip building block from a 4× 4× 4 3D Torus 
cube into a 2× 2× 2× 2× 2× 2 hyper-cube. In the current 3D design, each chip connects to 6 nearest 
neighbors across X/Y/Z axes and the cube exposes 16 optical ICI links on each of its 6 faces, totaling 
96 external optical links per cube (~1.5 optical ports per chip). Under the 6D design, each dimension 
has 64 external optical links (2⁶ chips, on e outward link per chip per dimension), for a total of 6 ×  64 
= 384 external optical links (~6 optical ports per chip). The per-chip optical port count thus scales from 
1.5 to 6. Combined with Scale -out at an estimated 1:2.5, this brings the aggregate TPU -to-port ratio 
to 1:8.5. 
Lower latency and closer to non -blocking Scale-up: For a 4,096 -chip superpod, a 3D torus (4× 4× 4) 
carries a worst-case diameter of 24 hops, whereas a 6D torus (2× 2× 2× 2× 2× 2) halves that to 12 hops 
(2× 6), directly compressing collective-operation and latency. Higher dimensionality also spreads traffic 
across 6 orthogonal paths, improving bisection bandwidth and moving closer to non-blocking chip-to-
chip communication. Critically, shortening each edge from 4 to 2 nodes eliminates deep mu lti-hop 
electrical paths inside the block: the architecture deliberately reduces intra -cube copper 
communication and pushes a far larger share of traffic onto optical fiber. 
Key beneficiaries: The exact TPU generation for the adoption of 6D torus and initi al penetration rate 
remain unclear. That said, whether NVIDIA moves toward CPO/NPO or Google expands from 3D to 
6D, both are Scale -up strategies aimed at enabling greater inter -chip communication. Key 
beneficiaries include the 2.4T Coherent Lite module and the OCS supply chain, such as MEMS-based 
optical switch vendors, PIC/laser/DSP suppliers, and fiber/cabling vendors supporting the larger 
reconfigurable lightwave fabric.

Risks 
1) Alternative technologies; 2) AI demand and capex slowdown; 3) IT demand slowdown; 4) product 
delays.

## Document 309: Global Strategy Views_ Competition for Capital

Source PDF: all report pdf/Global Strategy Views_ Competition for Capital.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-09-17
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1. There are two themes dominating our investor conversations: the impact of AI and 
the rise in interest rates. The two issues are linked as demand for capital from both 
the private and public sectors increase. In the private sector a surge in capex 
spending to fund AI infrastructure has eaten into free cash flow and forced 
companies to raise more in debt and equity markets. Meanwhile, government 
borrowing needs have increased as priorities shift towards upgrading critical 
infrastructure, energy security and defense at a time when cyclical inflationary 
pressures driven by higher energy prices are also resulting in higher policy rates. The 
combination has pushed up the cost of capital. As recently as 2022, for example, 
30-year bond yields in Germany and Japan were close to zero (Exhibit 1). A rise in 
yields, together with more uncertainty (over geopolitics and the future impact of AI) 
have, in combination, pushed up the cost of capital.

2. This rise in yields comes after a near-record period of equity outperformance 
relative to bonds over 10-year holding periods (Exhibit 2).

Exhibit 1: Rising yields and uncertainty have increased the cost of capital 
30-year government bond benchmark yields (%)

As equities have continued to outperform bonds, equity risk premia have fallen back to 
levels last seen in the late 1990s, leaving equity markets more vulnerable to further 
increases in bond yields. That said, in the US and Japan, the ERP has bounced off recent 
lows (Exhibit 3).

3. The impact of rising bond yields, however, depends not only on the level of yields, but 
also on the reasons as well as the speed of the adjustment (Exhibit 4). It is the speed 
issue that has caused the biggest impact in the recent past. As our US strategists note, 
stocks have usually generated positive returns alongside rising interest rates unless the 
pace of rising rates exceeded 2+ standard deviations and this is a pattern that is broadly

4. But despite the rise in yields, nominal GDP and profit growth remain strong and, until 
recently, the impact of rising bond yields has been offset by the strength in corporate 
profits. Earnings growth has been the main driver of equity returns over the past 18 
months in all regions (Exhibit 5). This strength has meant that PE multiples have either 
been flat (Japan and Europe) or have fallen (the US, Asia and EM).

the start of the year to 19x, in line with its long-run average, despite the market being 
close to its all-time high. 
5. While US equities have driven the bulk of equity outperformance for much of the 
fifteen years post the financial crisis, the improvement in earnings across regions, and 
the sharper de-rating in the US, has resulted in equity performance being 
broader-based by geography. Since 2025, the US equity market has been the weakest of 
the major regions (Exhibit 6), a turnaround from the dominant trend since the financial 
crisis and increasing investor returns from a more geographically diversified portfolio.

6. Supportive profit growth has been accompanied by positive earnings revisions, with 
2026 and 2027 estimates moving higher across major regions. There are four broad 
areas which have been driving much of this profit growth. First, technology earnings 
continue to be very robust. Second, rising energy prices have pushed up profits in the 
commodity sector. Third, banks have generally enjoyed strong earnings backed by 
positive nominal GDP growth, steep yield curves and strong private sector balance 
sheets. Finally, sectors such as Industrials have benefited from the surge in AI capex 
spending which has spilled over into improved revenues for the ‘pick and shovels’ of AI 
infrastructure. The breadth of the earnings’ growth has also increased the opportunity 
for investors to diversify across sectors as well as countries.

7. While technology profit growth has remained strong, the surge in capex spending 
among the hyperscalers has increasingly eaten through their free cash flow (Exhibit 8) 
prompting companies to look for alternative sources of funding, in the credit and equity 
markets.  The capex spending growth for AA-rated issuers has been substantial: the 65% 
year-over-year growth in Q2 marks the 10th consecutive quarter that aggregate AA 
capex growth exceeds 35%. They have also turned to the convertible bond market 
where volume has also increased year to date reaching $135 billion in the US, with 
AI-related borrowers driving 44% of total issuance, on our estimates. 
Our Credit team raised their full-year US IG gross issuance forecast by $200bn to $2.3 
trillion and AI-related issuers account for a quarter of USD IG gross supply this year, 
pushing overall supply to a year-to-date record.

### Page 7

surge. Globally technology has seen its PE ratio moderate, falling below its 20-year 
median (Exhibit 10). Meanwhile, the spending of these companies has spilled over into 
better earnings and more visible cash flows in many other parts of the economy. 
Healthcare, Consumer Discretionary and Industrials are all more expensive in absolute 
terms than IT, and Industrials has re-rated and now has a PE close to the 90th percentile 
of its 20-year range.

9. For these reasons, we have argued that Technology equities have not experienced a 
classic bubble because valuation levels are much lower than in previous financial 
bubbles. Nevertheless, the risk for investors is less about valuation and more whether 
there is an unsustainable earnings bubble. Bubbles in earnings, rather than valuations, 
have occurred in previous periods.  The Bank sector, for example, briefly became the 
biggest sector in the S&P 500 in the run up to the financial crisis of 2008/09 (Exhibit 11). 
Unlike the technology bubble of the late 1990s, or the Japanese bubble of the late 
1980s, Bank stocks did not experience a major valuation bubble at the time, but the 
surge in earnings that drove the sector’s outperformance proved to be unsustainable.

### Page 8

There are, however, important differences to the Tech sector today. The earnings of the 
banks were boosted by sharply rising leverage and the asset that they were financing – 
US real estate – did experience a valuation bubble. When the housing market collapsed, 
pushing economies into recession, it triggered a sharp fall in underlying earnings for the 
banking sector. There are important differences today. First, technology profits remain 
very robust, and balance sheets are strong overall. Second, interest coverage ratios for 
the aggregate S&P 500 and its median stock, for example, rank in the 99th and 68th 
percentiles relative to the past 20 years. Third, demand for compute is accelerating and 
outstripping supply; Microsoft recently declared their intent to triple data centre 
capacity in 6 years. The bottlenecks are increasingly power, land and data centre 
capacity rather than a lack of compute demand. At our annual Communacopia 
Technology Conference, Nvidia reiterated its 2030 AI TAM outlook to $3tn-$4tn and 
supply constraints continue to be broad based. Nonetheless, any slowdown in profit 
growth, in an environment of a much higher cost of capital, could put downward 
pressure on equity prices, reducing confidence in future cash flows across the ecosystem 
from the hyperscalers to the ‘pick and shovels’ that have been benefiting from the capex 
boom.  
10. Given the near-term risks in bond markets, we remain neutral on equities, alongside 
other asset classes on a 3-month horizon, but continue to be overweight equities over 
12 months. While profit growth is likely to slow from the recent strong pace, ongoing 
growth in nominal GDP should underpin equity markets and the earnings cycle 
propelling equity returns even without any valuation expansion from current levels and 
our economists continue to see a low risk of recession. As our asset allocation team has 
shown, earnings growth and ROE are likely to continue to be the key drivers of equity 
performance relative to bonds, and we expect these fundamentals to remain supportive 
for equity returns. Nevertheless, we continue to recommend well diversified equity 
exposure as opportunities continue to broaden across geographies, sectors and factors.

has also seen significant dispersion of returns. Differentiation, diversification and alpha 
generation are likely to remain the key drivers of equity returns. With higher bond yields, 
valuations are not likely to rise making earnings the critical driver of returns for investors.

## Document 310: How bad is the rise in bond yields_ (2026-09-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/How bad is the rise in bond yields_ (2026-09-23).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-23
Pages: 7

### Page 1

1

SPECIAL EDITION | HOW BAS IS THE RISE 
IN BOND YIELDS?

TRANSCRIPTION

0:00:10 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Everyone, welcome. As we head into autumn 2026, interest rates are reaching levels never seen for many, many years. 
Why is this happening? Is it sustainable? And what are the implications for the economies of different countries? All these 
questions will be discussed over the next few minutes with the economists of BNP Paribas. Let us begin with Isabelle 
Mateos y Lago, chief economist of the group. Hello, Isabelle.  
0:00:38 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
Hello Emmanuel, hello everyone. 
0:00:39 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you for being with us today. Just to begin, a simple explanation. What rates are we talking about and which countries 
are affected? 
0:00:48 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
Yes, it is very important to clarify this. So, we are talking about long-term interest rates, rates at 10 years or longer 
maturities, 30 years, etc. The rates at which sovereign states borrow on the market. And this is primarily something that is 
happening in advanced countries, so United States, United King dom, Japan, and then the Eurozone countries. And indeed, 
as you said, we are seeing since the beginning of the summer, interest rates reach levels we had not seen for about 20 
years. But it is important to keep in mind that up until that point, up until the mid -2000s, the level of interest rates that 
we're seeing now were really quite common, quite normal, so one shouldn't overdramatize.  
0:01:35 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Why do we find ourselves in this situation and how did we get to this point?  
0:01:41 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
Yes, so the direct cause is that investors are demanding higher interest rates in order to  lend their capital to governments. 
But why is this? Well, there's essentially three reasons for this, and not all of them are bad, and they're not mutually 
exclusive either. The top reason to my mind is that there's just greater competition for capital be tween sovereign states 
and the corporate sector, who are all facing simultaneously additional investment needs, in defense, in artificial 
intelligence, in energy transition, in resilience. And so,  you just have greater competition for capital and that pushes up the 
price of capital, which is the interest rate. 
Reason number two is because markets have revised upwards their expectations of how high central banks are going to be 
taking their interest rates. 
And this mechanically translates into higher rates at the long end. And then there's a third reason, which doesn't apply 
necessarily in every case, but which is essentially higher sovereign risk, a kind of a risk premium, in cases where there are  
concerns arising about the sustainability of these public debts.

### Page 2

2

0:02:59 - Emmanuel Laborde 
What are the implications for global economies? 
0:03:03 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
So that's exactly what we'll be discussing in the next segments. But basically, the two key issues are, one, an adverse 
impact on public finances. It's higher interest rates making it harder to balance the books in terms of public accounts. And 
then secondly, these higher rates tend to slow down economic growth. Of course, by how much and how serious it will be, 
depends on how high interest rates go and how long they stay there. But that's for the next segments.  
0:03:38 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Yes. Thank you very much, Isabelle. Having said the scene now, let's move on to the first segment with the following 
question. What makes debt sustainable? And let's see what Hélène Baudchon has to say.  
Hello Hélène and welcome into this special edition. 
0:03:58 - Hélène Baudchon 
Hello Emmanuel, hello everybody. 
0:04:01 - Emmanuel Laborde 
When economists talk about public debt, they often refer to the sustainability of debt. As an economist, can you explain to 
us what that means and what is the debt trajectory deemed unsustainable?  
0:04:15 - Hélène Baudchon 
To begin with, I will say that a temporary increase in debt can be seen as a normal evolution, not particularly concerning, 
for instance during an economic crisis or more recently during a health crisis. In such cases, it acts as a buffer. A problem  
arises when the debt level never comes back down. An unsustainable trajectory can be therefore defined, characterized by 
a continuous regular rise in the debt level, both in absolute terms and as a proportion of GDP, driven by high and recurring 
fiscal deficits. And sometimes interest payments can exacerbate the problem.  
0:05:00 - Emmanuel Laborde 
In very simple terms, you are talking about the snowball effect.  
0:05:03 - Hélène Baudchon 
Exactly explaining why consolidation measures are needed in order to prevent such an adverse situation from occurring 
and in order to prevent, in fact, a sudden loss of confidence from creditors.  
0:05:16 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Regarding governments, what should serve as a warning sign for them? And at what point could the situation become, let's 
say, problematic? 
0:05:27 - Hélène Baudchon 
Well, simply speaking, when interest rates rise, not so long ago, up until 2022, Eurozone countries were borrowing at very 
low rates and in some instance, even negative rates. And although debt levels were already at high level at that time, the 
low rates helped manage debt servicing. Not much fiscal effort was needed to keep the public debt ratios in check.  
0:05:55 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Of course, we all remember this because it was a very unusual situation, but now things are very different.

### Page 3

3

0:06:01 - Hélène Baudchon 
Yes, indeed, the situation today is clearly more challenging simply because bond yields are significantly higher and they 
are rising at the time of our discussion. This is for the part the result of the ECB's monetary tightening in 2022 -2023 aimed 
at addressing the inflation shock then. Then long-term interest rates have risen as a consequence and have aligned more 
closely with GDP growth rate. And in technical terms, this is what we refer to as a narrowing of the "r" - "g" spread. 
0:06:41 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Why is this spread so important? Why do you, the economists, monitor it this way?  
0:06:48 - Hélène Baudchon 
Essentially, you have the interest rate "r", which determines the debt burden, while the growth rate "g" determines the 
trajectory of fiscal revenues. And if "r" is lower than "g", and this has been the case for many years, as you can see on the  
chart on the screen, it means that interest payments are increasing at a slower pace than the revenue component of the 
equation. 
0:07:14 - Emmanuel Laborde 
And this provides some leeway in other budgetary areas, such as education, of course, defense, maybe pensions.  
0:07:22 - Hélène Baudchon 
Yes, indeed. And this is what we refer to as the primary budget. However, if and when interest rates do go up while the 
economy isn't growing that much faster, then fiscal leeway diminishes.  
0:07:35 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Crystal clear. Now, just could you provide a few last words to wrap up the sustainability segment?  
0:07:43 - Hélène Baudchon 
Well, to conclude, we are entering a period which demands greater fiscal discipline or, as some might say, less indiscipline 
in order to manage debt trajectories effectively, in particular in the countries which have high debt levels. And for instanc e, 
the Italian case is an interesting one because it is well known for its high debt level ratio. But in fact, it also has an 
advantageous position because it has no primary deficit. In fact, it even benefits from a large primary surplus.  
0:08:22 - Emmanuel Laborde 
As for the countries... For the other countries... I stop you because this will be a discussion with Guillaume Derrien during  
the next segment. Thank you very much, Hélène. 
0:08:30 - Hélène Baudchon 
Thank you, Emmanuel. 
0:08:36 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Hello, Guillaume. Welcome. 
0:08:37 - Guillaume Derrien 
Thank you. 
0:08:38 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Hello, Emmanuel, and hello to everyone. The flexibility of fiscal policy depends heavily on interest rates, which have 
increased considerably over the past few months. The question is, where do we stand today?

### Page 4

4

0:08:52 - Guillaume Derrien 
So, long-term rates have increased in all advanced economies, reaching levels not seen since 2008 in Europe and in the 
United States, and even since 1996 in Japan. Fortunately, the effective rate that states pay on their debt is lower than thos e 
headline figures suggest, simply because the average maturity of debt is relatively long. Precisely how long are we talking 
about? So, it's about 10 years in Japan, 13 years in the United Kingdom, and 8.5 years in France. That means that most of 
the debt was raised before rates started climbing. 
For example, in France, the effective rate just recently rose above the 2% mark, which is significantly lower than the 10 -
year bond yield, which exceeded 4% for new issuances.  
That said, in the United States, which has an average maturity of roughly six years, is experiencing a more rapid increase in  
its effective rate. 
0:09:54 - Emmanuel Laborde 
And this rate increase is happening in the context of high debt -to-GDP ratios and with modest growth, correct? Yes, that's 
correct. 
0:10:05 - Guillaume Derrien 
Debt levels have reached unprecedented heights since the end of World War II. And at the same time, growth is lower, 
hovering around 2% on average in the United States, 1% in Europe and 0.5% in Japan. What does that mean for debt 
servicing costs? So, debt servicing cost is already relatively high, but it is expected to grow further as debt issue before the 
2022 rate hikes mature and are replaced with new instruments bearing higher interest rates. By 2030, interest costs could 
rise to levels last seen in 2000, specifically in France, around 4% of GDP. 
Are all the countries equally affected by these trends? Actually, no. Some countries are managing to reduce their debt 
level. 
Spain, Italy, even Japan have sufficiently improved their primary balance to achieve this, at least for the time being. And o f 
course, we must speak about Germany. Yes, Germany is implementing investment programs that result in a higher public 
deficit and debt-to-GDP ratio. But this ratio started from a relatively low base at around above 60% of GDP. That said, in 
this current context, German bonds have risen. And because German bonds are used as a benchmark for Europe, it  raises 
rates across all Eurozone countries. Is France the only country seeing its public debt go up?  
No, it's not, Emmanuel. This is happening in the United Kingdom and in the United States. These economies, like France, 
are running public deficits that are ever close or exceed 5% of GDP, which is too large for debt ratio to stabilize on its ow n. 
France and the United Kingdom are trying to gradually reduce their public deficit so as not to compromise growth. The 
United States is not going on that path yet, but market pressure could eventually force them to do so.  
0:12:08 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you very much for your input, Guillaume. Thank you, Emmanuel. Let's go to the next segment. It's about the impact 
of rising bond yields on financing and investments in the Eurozone.  
0:12:26 - Thomas Humblot 
Hello, Thomas. Welcome. Hello. Thank you for having me today.  
0:12:30 - Emmanuel Laborde 
We've just seen what's behind the rise in Eurozone bond yields. This rise is partly attributed to the sovereign spread. What 
impact will this have on the economy? 
0:12:42 - Thomas Humblot 
Actually, this widening of some government bond spreads is obviously not good news, but it tends paradoxically to have 
less impact than an increase in long-term interest rates that does not include the sovereign risk premium. for example, in 
France, for a very long time, it has been considered that the 10 -year OAT was the relevant benchmark for fixed loans. And 
at that time, the spread on French government bonds was relatively stable. But since 2024, that spread increased

### Page 5

5

significantly and that renders the sovereign yield a less relevant benchmark.  
And now the most relevant benchmark is the swap rate because it determines the cost of bank funding that backs new 
loans. 
0:13:56 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Since the end of 2024, we have seen a rise in long-term 10-year swap rates. What impact does this have on bank lending 
rates? 
0:14:08 - Thomas Humblot 
Fixed loans have obviously become more expensive, but to a lesser extent than if they were priced solely based on long -
term sovereign rates. And what about existing fixed-rate loans? 
There is no impact. An increase in rates for the entire maturity of a loan does not have any effect if it is a fixed -rate loan. 
Borrowers with outstanding loans are therefore protected. Obviously, new fixed loans are granted at higher rates. And this 
sharply contrasts with a floating-rates loans. 
Because with floating-rates loans, borrowers experience regular rate adjustments throughout the term of the loan. And in 
terms of bank financing? Strictly in terms of bank financing, an increase in short -term rates has an immediate and 
widespread restrictive impact on borrowers, while for long-term interest rates, the impact is more gradual, and it comes 
gradually as the outstanding amount of fixed-rate loans are progressively renewed. And the thing is that since the 
beginning of the war in Iran, we have seen a combination of the two.  
And in practical terms, the rate of new bank loans to non-financial corporations has increased about 25 basis points in the 
eurozone since the beginning of the year. 
0:16:12 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Just a few words about companies that borrow directly from the market.  
0:16:16 - Thomas Humblot 
Well, in that case, the impact is even more straightforward. The rate at which a company borrows from the market is the 
risk-free rate plus a premium. 
0:16:28 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Have risk premiums, known as corporate credit spreads, also increased?  
0:16:34 - Thomas Humblot 
Yes, they have increased temporarily at the beginning of the year but have returned to normal since. For the time being, the 
widening remains moderate for the highest-rated investment-grade issuers. 
However, some significant bond issuance in the euro debt market due to some major US technology companies has 
increased spreads for longer maturities. 
0:17:11 - Emmanuel Laborde 
In particular terms, how does this affect the investment decisions of the companies?  
0:17:19 - Thomas Humblot 
Well, investments that were previously worthwhile due to returns sufficient to cover the cost of capital may no longer do so 
and in turn that might lead to a decrease in demand for financing.  
0:17:40 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you very much Thomas. Now let us move on to the emerging economies. And it is with Cynthia Kalasopatan -Antoine.

### Page 6

6

Hello Cynthia, and welcome. 
0:17:54 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Hi, Emmanuel. Hello, everyone. 
0:17:57 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Long-term bond yields rose significantly in advanced economies over the summer. What can we now say about emerging 
markets? 
0:18:06 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Emerging bond markets held up well, which is quite unusual. The rise in long -term sovereign yields in emerging countries 
has been less pronounced than in advanced economies, including the United States and Germany. Now, there are two 
main exceptions, Turkey and Poland. Meanwhile, we did not witness any significant correction in emerging financial 
markets over the summer, and the main currencies appreciated slightly against the dollar or the euro.  
0:18:37 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Does this mean that public finances are more robust in emerging countries?  
0:18:43 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Well, it depends. You see, in cases like the Czech Republic and South Korea, public debt is moderate and macroeconomic 
fundamentals are solid. However, these countries are now less frequently considered as emerging and tend to be viewed 
as newly advanced economies. By contrast, Brazil and South Africa have weaker public finances and investors have 
already incorporated a higher sovereign risk into their assessments. And this is reflected in the bond yields. And this 
assessment of sovereign risk did not really change over the summer. 
0:19:26 - Emmanuel Laborde 
When comparing the main fiscal metrics, what are the trends? What do you see as an economist?  
0:19:33 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
On average, the fiscal metrics in emerging markets are not substantially worse than those in advanced economies. The 
average budget deficit levels are similar, ranging from 4% to 5% of GDP in 2025 -2026, according to the IMF. And as in 
advanced economies, the public debt is higher than its pre-Covid level. However, the average public debt to GDP ratio in 
emerging countries excluding China is close to 60%, whereas in advanced economies, it is above 100%.  
0:20:12 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Do you arrive at the same conclusion when comparing debt sustainability criteria?  
0:20:17 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Well, there are significant regional differences. In emerging Asia, the factors that determine debt sustainability are more 
often favorable than those in advanced economies. In Central Europe, the gap between the interest rate paid on debt and 
the GDP growth rate is also favorable. However, the primary deficits in some countries are high.  
0:20:45 - Emmanuel Laborde 
In a few words, a brief overview of the situation in Latin America.  
0:20:49 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Well, Brazil and Mexico are in a less favorable position. These countries would indeed need to generate a significant 
primary budget surplus each year in order to stabilize their debt in the medium term.

### Page 7

7

0:21:07 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Economists use the term original sin, which refers to the situation when governments resort to borrowing in foreign 
currency to address the shortfall in domestic financing. Does this phenomenon continue to distinguish emerging 
countries? 
0:21:24 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Yes, this is still the case. Emerging countries still have a significant share of their debt in foreign currency. This repres ents 
about 35 to 40% of their total debt on average. And in fact, now, this figure has decreased over the past decade, and this 
contributes to their resilience. However, governments in emerging markets have a less favorable position as regards their 
debt profile and their financing costs are also on average higher.  
0:22:02 - Emmanuel Laborde 
And finally, just a few words about a larger subject, China.  
0:22:07 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
China's public debt is significant and growing rapidly. That being said, it is denominated in domestic currency and issued in  
domestic markets. This debt is, however, sustainable in the medium term due to a very low effective interest rate and a 
high growth rate. 
0:22:29 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you very much, Cynthia, for being with us today.  
0:22:32 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Thank you. 
0:22:33 - Emmanuel Laborde 
It is now time to conclude, and we welcome back Group Chief Economist Isabelle Mateos y Lago.  
It is time for our conclusion with Isabelle. Once again, just a final question, please. Could or should, I do not know which 
word is the good one, central banks intervene to curb this rise in interest rates?  
0:22:58 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
Absolutely not. The main contribution that central banks can bring to dealing with this situation is to restore price stabili ty, 
as is their mandate, as promptly as possible. And in the current circumstances, this means keeping relatively high policy 
rates, in some cases, rising them further. And the responsibility for keeping public finances on a sustainable footing rests 
squarely with governments and any attempt by these governments to instead take the easy way out by pressuring central 
banks to lower interest rates prematurely would be enormously counterproductive.  
0:23:41 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you very much, Isabelle. Thank you all for joining us today. Just a few words to remind you that additional economic 
research content can be found by scanning the QR code shown on your screen. Do not hesitate to use it with your

TAKING STOCK

The Reserve Bank of India (RBI) has adopted a 
multi-layered defence strategy to support the 
rupee this year.

This has entailed a combination of outright 
intervention in spot/ forwards, regulatory 
action, liquidity steps, administrative measures 
and moves to curb import -related dollar 
demand.

The rupee has appreciated by 1.8% against the 
dollar in the past five days since recent 
measures took effect, paring YTD depreciation 
to 5.5% . High oil prices, weakness in capital 
inflows, a firm dollar , tariffs/ geopolitical 
worries, energy , and risk -off sentiments have 
kept the INR under pressure since last year.

                                                                                                                                            2

ECHOES OF 2013 WITH FEW 
DIFFERENCES

Comparisons with 2013 are apt, though 
the backdrop differs.  As for the 
catalysts, markets reacted to the US 
Fed’s taper announcement in 2013, 
which pushed US yields higher  and led 
global investors to reduce exposure to 
vulnerable EM economies. As a result, 
capital inflows weakened, exposing 
funding mismatch es for countries, 
including India, that were running wide 
current account deficits.

In 2026, markets face the spectre of 
elevated oil prices, geopolitical 
uncertainty, global competition for 
capital, a firm dollar ( DXY appreciated 
+3.8% YTD) , and US inflation/ deficits 
which have settled at levels much higher 
than pre -pandemic years. This has 
pushed up UST yields, and the US Fed 
kicked off its rate-hike cycle in Sep26.

Against this backdrop, the RBI is 
pursuing the same objective , i.e., to 
ensure rupee stability, but the nature of 
the battle differs. The defence strategy 
has shifted from ‘crisis management’ 
mode in 2013 , due to weaker domestic 
macroeconomic balances (a wide current 
account gap, high inflation, a lower 
reserve buffer, and wide fiscal deficits) , 
to a ‘terms-of-trade shock and impact of 
a firm dollar’ in 2026.

Flashpoints are  nonetheless similar, 
with reliance on portfolio financing , 
leaving the external account vulnerable 
to shifts in global risk sentiment. 
Portfolio outflows have emerged as the 
primary transmission channel through 
which external shocks have translated 
into INR depreciation pressure , 
compounded by high energy prices and a 
firm dollar.

FX PLAYBOOK

While the response framework is similar 
between 2013 and this year, the broad 
sequence and nature of macroprudential 
measures differ. This year’s multi-
layered rupee defence toolkit has 
included:

-Direct FX defence  by way of 
intervention in the spot and forwards 
market. As of Aug26, the net forward 
dollar liabilities ballooned to $200bn, 
rising by ~$63bn vs . the month before . 
Recent sell/buy FX swaps are expected 
to lower this stock in the coming months.

-Attracting dollar inflows  by way of 
special swap facilities for FCNR deposits, 
and offshore borrowings , apart from 
liberalising taxes around debt inflows,

### Page 3

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            3

amongst others. We discussed this here, 
here, here and here.

Taking a proactive stance, a portion of 
existing FX reserves could be earmarked 
against forward liabilities (from maturing 
FCNR deposits) , helping to mitigate 
concerns that deposit maturities or debt 
repayments could trigger a sharp
increase in dollar ⁠demand down the line.

-Domestic liquidity management. Bonds, 
to some degree, are paying the price for 
the authorities' efforts to defend the 
rupee against the additional headwind of 
weak global conditions.  INR liquidity 
surged on the back of the swap windows, 
prompting the RBI to undertake open 
market operations, VRRR auctions, sell -
buy swaps, among others. While these 
have been effective in absorbing 
balances, bond yields have drifted higher. 
10Y yields are at three-year highs and are 
expected to climb further  (DBSf: 7.40 -
7.50% by year-end).

-Moderate FX demand and speculation . 
Earlier in the year, the RBI had 
introduced change s to the banks’ net 
open position framework, and thereafter 
partially rolled back a few curbs. 
Prudential measures on FX exposures 
and hedging -related measures were 
announced in Oct26, which include d the 
introduction of a daily cash reserve with 
the RBI equal to 20% of the rupee 
equivalent of the notional value for INR -
linked derivative contracts exceeding 
$2mn used to hedge current account 
transactions, referred to as the Foreign 
Exchange Risk Reserve (FERR) . 
Subsequent clarifications provided 
operational details on where the FERR 
would be maintained and that it would be 
subject to audits.

Since 12 Oct, the RBI has been providing 
dollars via designated banks to the three 
state-owned oil -marketing companies 
(Indian Oil, BPCL, and HPCL) to meet the 
their daily dollar requirements, thereby 
diverting related spot-market demand.

On hedging-related matters, the limit for 
undertaking foreign exchange derivative 
transactions without proving an 
underlying commercial exposure was 
cut to $5mn from $100mn across all ADs 
and exchange -traded currency 
derivatives. Restrictions on Rebooking , 
i.e., a uthorized dealers are prohibited 
from allowing customers to rebook 
cancelled rupee -linked derivative 
contracts (though rollovers upon 
maturity remain permitted). Lastly, 
banks must obtain written confirmation 
from users that the same underlying 
exposure has not been hedged with 
another dealer.  Data from CCI , cited by

### Page 4

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            4

Bloomberg, pointed to importers’ 
forward dollar purchases rising 44% on 
the year to a monthly average of $64bn.

-Current account management . Efforts 
to contain structural FX outflows by way 
of higher duties and taxes on gold 
imports discourage non -essential 
purchases and production incentives to 
improve export competitiveness.

IMPACT

To assess whether the playbook has 
worked, we observe:

a) Rupee has enjoyed a brief respite. 
Despite oil prices at $105/bbl at the time 
of writing and elevated global yields, 
USDINR fell from near 96.80 to wards 
95.00, owing to measures announced in 
Oct26 following the central bank’s 25bp 
hike. Beyond temporary relief, global 
catalysts are likely to be back to the fore 
and weigh on the currency (see chart);

b) Sharp inflows under the swap 
facilities pushed the foreign reserves 
stock to a record high, providing 
policymakers sufficient buffer to 
navigate future shocks from a position of 
strength. A strong interventionist 
response has since led to a softening in 
the overall stock (see chart).

c) Macroprudential measures have led to 
distortions in other parts of the market . 
For instance, strong swap -related 
inflows had inorganically pushed 
onshore INR liquidity up sharply, 
necessitating absorption measures. 
Similarly, NOP restrictions and the more 
recent tighter requirements on hedging 
activity and counterparty transactions  
had some after -effects. Forward points 
have surged this week , with financial 
institutions pointing to relative illiquidity 
in the currency forwards market  on 
volumes and activity basis, and fewer 
avenues to deal with dollar supply.

### Page 5

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            5

d) Swap-related windows are a timely 
support for the country’s balance of 
payments position. From expectations of 
a third consecutive year of BOP deficit in 
FY27, the math has now swung to a 
potential surplus, even as current 
account worries remain. With energy 
prices settling north of $100/bbl, 
keeping the monthly trade deficit near 
$30bn, our FY27 current account deficit 
forecast of 1.1% of GDP faces  upside 
risks of around 0.3-0.5% of GDP. We note 
the s harp jump in ECB outflows in S ep, 
though see that as a temporary overshot 
of leverage-FCNR activity.

WHAT LIES AHEAD

Authorities are likely not done yet and 
remain ready to take further action, as 
required. RBI Governor Malhotra 
reiterated the authorities’ serious intent 
of stabilising the currency in a recent 
interview (Moneycontrol, 13 Oct), 
emphasising that “The RBI is determined 
to do whatever is necessary to ensure 
orderly movement in the foreign 
exchange market,”. He also highlighted 
that recent steps were not tantamount to 
capital control measures that were 
aimed at ensuring that forex markets 
serve genuine hedging needs rather than 
speculative bets.  Market participants 
opine that FX volumes and interest are 
likely to be subdued in a few pockets on 
the back of the recent measures, 
consequently stabilizing the rupee.

Unlike in 2013, when policy tightening 
was a part of the initial response, the 
2026 strategy has prioritised targeted 
FX and regulatory measures.  The 
sequencing suggests a deliberate effort 
to distinguish actions aimed at 
preserving currency stability from those 
intended to achieve domestic monetary 
objectives, with rates remaining a 
potential, though not immediate, tool in 
the defence framework.

However, global developments have 
altered that dynamic . Rising US yields 
and a gradual increase in the US Fed 
Funds rate (DBS baseline: a total of 75bp 
hikes) have compressed IN -US spreads. 
The recent sell -off in INR benchmark

### Page 6

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            6

bonds has effectively widened Gsec -
UST spreads across maturities, in 
contrast to the compression witnessed in 
regional high-yielders.

The latest announcements may not 
fully quell speculation over further 
policy action.

Upcoming measures might include -

-Markets anticipate relief on the long -
term capital gains tax structure on 
equities to draw in longer-duration 
inflows. This measure will boost inflows 
if it coincides with other factors that 
make domestic equities attractive for 
thematic investing , as global risk -free 
rates moderate.

-Street expectations also include steps 
to boost inward dollar repatriation by 
exporters to boost domestic dollar 
supply.

-Wider policy rate corridor akin to 2013.

-Policy rate response. While this might 
not be a panacea, it is a likely necessary 
condition. Raising the neutral rate might 
have mixed success in attracting capital 
market inflows ; though rate-sensitive 
flows are likely to get a fillip as investors 
seek attractive rate differentials in the 
region.

An adequate real rate cushion would 
also align with the evolving domestic 
inflation realities, which are expected to 
accelerate to 6% in the Oct-Dec quarter 
and remain above 5% well into 2027.

Given fluid global developments, we 
outline three scenarios on the rate path 
ahead. Our baseline assumption  is for 
75bp hikes in this cycle  (including Oct), 
spread between the Dece mber and 
February 2027 meetings. We don’t rule 
out an intermeeting rate hike before the 
December meeting, contingent on 
rupee movements.

Under the second scenario , s hould 
depreciation pressures on the rupee 
return, the 50bp hike might be brought 
forward to December , with another in 
February, taking cumulative hikes in this 
cycle to 100bp. The t hird scenario  is a 
more aggressive 150bp worth of hikes in 
this cycle, with 50bp front -loaded in 
December, and the rest spilling into 2027. 
Factors that  will determine whether 
these scenarios materialise are the 
resumption and scale of rupee 
depreciation, oil price direction, US Fed 
Funds rate increases (DBSf: total 75bps) 
and other risk-off events.

Path ahead

Despite these measures, we see limited 
room for sustained INR appreciation on 
grounds of manufacturing/ trade 
competitiveness. A challenging flows 
portfolio flows outlook leaves the d oor 
open to further gradual rupee 
depreciation subject to global 
developments, with high sensitivity to oil 
price movements and risk sentiments . A 
strong rate response will help introduce  
favourable rate differentials and reduce 
currency volatility.

2027 is likely to be another eventful year for European utilities.
Geopolitical uncertainty, volatility in energy markets and
signiﬁcant investment pipelines, together with the associated
funding needs, should remain key drivers for the sector going into
next year.

for full-year 2026. Net supply also remains ﬁrmly positive at €35bn

next year, although somewhat below the €44bn in 2026.

Alongside redemptions, bond supply continues to be driven by
large capital expenditure. We expect combined investment to top
€200bn next year for large European utility issuers. German names

have the highest investment needs for 2027, up around 30% year-
on-year, while Italian, UK, and Dutch names are also among those
seeing a clear increase.
The energy transition continues to drive signiﬁcant investment
needs, with growing electriﬁcation and renewable generation
requiring substantial expansion and reinforcement of transmission
and distribution grids. Regulated utilities are particularly impacted
by higher grid investments, but integrated utilities are also likely to

see higher capital expenditure next year.

The US 10yr yield gapped back below 5% with some conviction.
We're not convinced it keeps on going. More likely it ultimately
reverts up again. Brent oil pushed above $110 for the ﬁrst time
since May, but weaker market sentiment prevented a hawkish
repricing. French bond spreads continue to surge, and we
struggle to see catalysts to push against this

Padhraic Garvey, CFA, Michiel Tukker and Benjamin Schroeder
10-year French government bonds are now yielding above 5%, whereas the
US 10-year Treasury has dipped below 5%
The US 10yr back below 5% - let's see how
it feels down there
The US 10yr yield broke back below 5% around midday in New York
trading on Thursday. And did so with a degree of conviction. We
thought 5% could hold, but always acknowledged that a
heightened (and still heightening) risk-oﬀ tone could in the end
dominate, at least for the near term. The fact that the French 10yr
has gone the other way and shot above 5% in recent days, and the
spread out to 175bp vs Germany, is also of relevance. We also note

that Thursday was the ﬁrst day of pronounced ﬂight into Bunds
since the French wobbles took hold. Treasuries have shared in this.
Whether we remain below 5% is a whole other question. We're
minded to expect it to snap back above, as we still have inﬂation /
ﬁscal deﬁcit / AI boom underpinnings to navigate. And we have the

It's also true that there has been a tendency shown in recent weeks
to buy into concessional 5+% levels for the US 10yr. We saw that in
spades post the recent poor CPI number, which was followed by an
immediate sell-oﬀ in Treasuries, but then an even bigger

subsequent rally. In a way, the latest break below 5% is a
culmination of that move. Now that 5% has been breached below,
where next?
More pertinently, can the market ﬁnd comfort at sub 5% levels
given the contemporaneous inﬂation dynamic. And, can the
market really ignore the fact that neither side has a plan for the
ﬁscal deﬁcit as we roll into the mid-term elections in the next
couple of weeks. One thing is for sure: the risk-oﬀ environment
takes pressure oﬀ the Fed to hike next week. But even given that,

we'd not be loading up on duration here, at least not just yet.
French downgrade lurking and risk
sentiment not helping French spreads either
Amid all this, French bonds remain in the line of ﬁre with the spread
over Bunds widening at times by 15bp, with the spread touching
177bp. That is still below its late-2011 peak of just under 200bp,
but at this pace it is only a matter of days before we see a new
high. Although a lot has changed in the eurozone since 2011, some

may consider Italy’s spreads during the sovereign debt crisis the
better yardstick since it was the country in market focus back then
and not France.

That said, the latest widening seems to be more driven by the

widening yet.
One event that market observers have been looking forward to is
the scheduled rating review by Moody’s after markets close on
Friday. Moody’s still has France on Aa3 with a negative outlook, and
therefore one notch above the other two major rating agencies that
place France already at A+, even with stable outlooks for France. A
downgrade from Moody’s now looks overdue and should be
considered a catch-up. Commentary will be parsed for further

downgrade triggers, since the developments in France – in terms of
political stability, public resistance and reﬁnancing costs – oﬀer
little hope of improvement anytime soon. In terms of timing, note
that there is no ﬁxed release time. When Moody’s downgraded
France in 2024, the news hit the wires only just after midnight CET.
Having at least this one risk event out of the way might bring about
some stabilisation. But any lasting relief would need positive news.

A budget for 2027 with at least some semblance of ﬁscal
consolidation would be a start, but might also have to wait until
December.
Friday’s events and market view
PMI data on Friday will be the next test for markets. So far,
both equity markets and economic data point to a resilient
economic outlook, but this narrative is challenged by rising
rates and higher oil prices. Concerns about AI valuations are
a recurring daily theme while energy prices keep drifting

higher. If we were to also see headwinds materialising in the
economic data, we could face quite a bullish repricing in
rates. Especially for euro rates, a more downbeat growth

台灣股市｜科技

TPCA 2026 quick takeaways

大尺寸、高層數、整合多顆內埋 Bridge / 矽電容、多層芯板 
1.高階載板:大尺寸、高層數、整合內埋元件（Bridge / 矽電容） 、多層芯板 
2. EMIB-T 載板需極高對位精度 
3. CPO（Co-Packaged Optics）IC 載板扮演著極關鍵的高密度整合平台角色

高階載板: 大尺寸、高層數、整合多顆內埋元件（Bridge / 矽電容） 、多層芯板 
AI 資料中心持續提升運算效能，主要面臨高速互連、高效供電及熱管理三大技術挑
戰，隨著晶片整合規模擴大、功耗增加，PCB 與 IC 載板必須朝更大尺寸、更高密度
及更多層數發展，並同步提升訊號完整性、電源完整性及翹曲控制能力。 隨著 AI 
GPU、CPO（光電共封裝）與高階 Networking Switch（網路交換器）的晶片尺寸
與功耗爆發，載板尺寸正朝向「極大尺寸（Extreme Large Body Size） 」發展。極
大尺寸必須搭配加厚的 Multi-Layer Core（如 2 層、3 層至 6 層 Core） ，以提供
足夠的結構剛性。整合多顆內埋元件（Bridge / 矽電容）可進一步化電源與訊號完
整性。先進封裝及伺服器系統加速升級，也對 IC 載板提出更高技術要求。

載板尺寸正朝向「極大尺寸（Extreme Large Body Size） 」發展。 
在高階載板（IC Substrates）產業中，隨著 AI GPU、CPO（光電共封裝）與高階 
Networking Switch（網路交換器） 的晶片尺寸與功耗爆發 ，Body Size（載板尺寸）
正朝向「極大尺寸（Extreme Large Body Size） 」發展。過去 100 x 100 mm 即被
視為極大尺寸；目前業界已進入 110 x 110 mm 量產階段，並推進至 150 x 130 
mm 與 150 x 150 mm（如 CPO 載板） 的樣品開發與驗證階段。針對未來高效能 
Switch 與 CPO 產品，載板尺寸已進一步升級至 230 x 230 mm 的極限規格。當
尺寸擴大至 100 x 100 mm 以上時，載板總層數普遍達到 15~20 層以上；若推
進至 230 x 230 mm，整體結構更需要高達 30 多層（如 16 + 2 + 16 ） 。極大尺
寸必須搭配加厚的 Multi-Layer Core（如 2 層、3 層至 6 層 Core） ，以提供足夠
的結構剛性，並增設電源/接地層 （Power/Ground） 以維護電源完整性 （PI） 。在大
尺寸載板上，業界不再追求極端微細線路。因為面積足夠容納線路，過細過密的線
路反而容易引發訊號干擾；研發重心已轉向「大尺寸、高層數、整合多顆內埋元件
（Bridge / 矽電容） 」 。

圖表 1: IC substrate , EBS( extreme large body size)

資料來源: Unimicron

### Page 2

台灣股市｜科技 
事件分析

Advanced substrate - Embedded Passive 埋入式被動元件載板 
1)因應高功耗需求：隨著 AI GPU 等高算力晶片的功耗與用電量急劇增加，晶片需
要更龐大且穩定的電力供應; 2)大幅縮短傳輸距離：傳統設計中，電源需由載板最底
層走較長的路徑傳遞至上層晶片；埋入矽電容後，電能可預先儲存在電容中並近距
離直接供電，傳輸路徑顯著縮短 以及 3)優化電源與訊號完整性：近距離供電能有
效提供更穩定的電源供應，優化  Power Integrity（PI，電源完整性） 與 Signal 
Integrity（SI，訊號完整性） ，促進電訊號傳遞。 IC substrate (Flip Chip BGA) 
Embedded Passive 可改善電源完整性（PI）與縮短供電路徑。矽電容主要埋設於
載板的增層 （Build-up layer） 結構中。位置偏向接近載板最上層，以便最直接地為
上方晶片提供電力支援 。Roadmap 顯示 2026 量產結構為 10/2/10; 8/6/8，NPI 為
18/2/18; 10/n/10，2027 為 14/n/14，2028 為 16/n/16，最大板身從 93x93mm
成長至 150x150mm。

圖表 2: IC substrate ,Embedded passive

資料來源: Unimicron

EMIB-T 載板需極高對位精度 
Bridge / Interposer（矽橋/中介層）埋入載板內部以提升晶片間傳輸效率。主要技
術門檻: 1) 極高對位精度 （Alignment）：放置 Bridge 等微小元件時，必須精確與
上下層的微細線路及盲孔 （Via） 對齊，對位稍有偏差即失效。2)異材質結合與可靠
度（Material Bonding & Reliability）：矽元件與載板有機材料間的接合面容易產生
氣泡 ， 在後續熱驗證中易引發分層 （Delamination） 問題 。3)不可重工 （No Rework）
與高成本 ：元件一旦埋入便無法取出一重工；若對位或接合不良導致瑕疵，整塊  
Panel/載板即宣告報廢，產生極高的成本風險 ; 4) 事前檢測（Known Good Die, 
KGD）： 為避免將不良元件埋入導致載板報廢 ， 元件在嵌入前必須先通過嚴格的良率
檢測 。5)多顆矽橋 （Multi-Bridge） 倍增的良率挑戰:隨著高階晶片尺寸 （Body Size），
載板內部需要同時內埋多顆 Bridge 元件。多顆內埋使對位與結合難度呈倍數級上
升。

### Page 3

台灣股市｜科技 
事件分析

Multi-Layer Core（多層芯板 / 多層 Core） 載板 
為高階載板（IC Substrate ）為因應高效能運算與 AI 晶片需求所採用的核心結構
技術。與傳統單一大塊單層 Core 的設計不同，Multi-Layer Core 是將兩層、三層
或更多層的 Core（C） ，中間透過 PP（Prepreg，半固化片） 黏合疊加在一起，使
載板中間的芯板結構顯著加厚。Multi-Layer Core（多層芯板 / 多層 Core） 載板 
可帶來, 1)電源完整性（PI）與訊號完整性（SI）：因應 AI GPU 與伺服器晶片功耗
及用電量大增，多層 Core 能設置更多 Power / Ground（電源層與接地層） 來分
流電流 ， 為單一晶片提供更穩定充足的電力 ， 優化 PI 與 SI。2) 結構強度與板彎控
制（Warping & Planarity）：加厚的 Core 結構能提升整體載板的剛性與平整度，
協助抵御熱應力與板彎形變 。雖然市場曾討論 「Core 變厚是否需轉用 Glass Core」，
但 Glass Core 現階段面臨易碎、價格高昂且缺乏成熟量產供應鏈的限制，因此現
階段高階載板仍以 Multi-Layer Core 的有機材料疊合方案為主。

圖表 3: IC substrate, Multi-layer core

資料來源: Unimicron

CPO（Co-Packaged Optics）IC 載板扮演著極關鍵的高密度整合平台角色 
由於 CPO 載板周邊需要配置光學模組與多種週邊零件，導致其 Body Size（載板
尺寸）比一般 GPU 載板更大，且趨勢是越做越大。目前 CPO 載板正積極推進至 
150 x 150 mm （目前多處於開發與送樣驗證階段） ；未來針對高階 Networking 
Switch，尺寸更可延伸至 230 x 230 mm 的極限規格。配合大尺寸設計，CPO 載
板的層數普遍落在 15~20 層以上 （甚至高達 20 幾層至 30 幾層） 。CPO 載板集
成了極大尺寸（Large Body Size） 、多層芯板（Multi-Layer Core） 、微孔（Micro 
Via）以及內埋元件（Embedded Bridge / 矽電容）等多項高階技術。雖然光學連
接器型態可能改變，但載板本身依然不可或缺，持續作為晶片與光電模組間供電與
訊號承載的核心基底。

《歐元投資關鍵-歐元區經濟活動加速擴張》
7月以來，北約示警灰色地帶模糊戰的風險升溫，導
致歐元短線偏弱 ，歐洲資金明顯轉向瑞郎進行避險 。
然而，歐洲基本面此時卻持續改善。歐元區9月景氣
加速擴張 ，綜合PMI初值創41個月新高 ，服務業與
製造業同步改善 ，其中製造業產出創 55個月高點。
德國成長動能增強 ，法國則10個月來首度恢復擴張 。
新訂單連續第3個月成長，增速為2022年5月以來最
快，出口訂單亦持續回升，帶動未完成訂單自2022
年6月以來首度增加。另一方面，中東衝突推升能源

價格，使投入成本與售價增速升至近4個月高點。整
體而言，歐元區經濟韌性優於預期 ，但通膨升溫與
企業信心偏弱仍是後續關注焦點。
貨幣政策方面 ，歐洲央行官員持續釋出偏鷹訊號 ，
認為能源價格再度上漲將推升並延長通膨壓力 ，未
來數月歐元區通膨可能接近4%。愛爾蘭央行行長表
示不排除10月再度升息，強調每次會議皆有行動可
能。首席經濟學家 Lane指出第 2輪能源衝擊將帶動
食品與商品價格上漲 。多位官員認為經濟仍具韌性 ，
為進一步收緊政策提供空間 ，市場也預期未來一年
歐洲央行仍有多次升息機會 ，以防止通膨預期根深
蒂固。基本面繼續支持 ECB升息的環境下 ，預計美
歐利差收斂將提供歐元支撐。4Q26目標價1.19。

長動能放緩。綜合PMI初值創6月以來新低，製造業
與服務業活動同步降溫 。其中，服務業受內需疲弱
及地緣政治不確定性拖累；製造業則受惠於 AI投資
與國防支出增加 ，但消費需求疲弱仍構成壓力 。通
膨壓力持續升溫，企業投入成本創6月以來最高，並
推升企業售價增幅 。英國經濟維持低速擴張 ，GDP
季增率僅約0.1%，但成本與通膨壓力升高 ，反映經
濟成長疲弱與通膨升溫並存的局面。
貨幣政策方面，9月央行維持利率3.75%不變，為成

熟國家少數維持利率不變的國家，英鎊兌美元創3個
月新低。央行總裁貝里表示 ，能源成本上升對英國
物價和薪資影響有限 ，但若這種波動持續下去 ，未
來有可能需要調高基準利率 。目前消費者物價指數
為3.1%，低於4月央行預測值 ，實際通膨展望低於
預期提供央行維持貨幣政策的基調 。展望後續，歐
非中東地緣政治情勢短期仍處於高不確定性階段 ，
並且導致近期天然氣價格飆升 ，英國仍有升息壓力 。
英國貨幣政策短線持穩 ，而經濟數據面則小幅改善 ，
但財政政策不確定性風險大幅削弱英鎊的投資誘因 ，
維持英鎊中性評等，英鎊兌美元年底目標1.34。

• 日本干預匯市動用15.4兆日圓
《日圓投資關鍵-日圓回貶但資金動能大幅轉正》
美元兌日圓自6-7月維持在160以上，7月底站上164
後大幅拉回 ，2026年7-8月份日本干預匯市共動用
15.4兆日圓，創2022年以來最高金額。8月美日聯手
干預日圓以來一度回測 160，但9月1日再度從160.4
快速下探至152.9，兩周日圓升值幅度近5%。市場揣
測日本政府年金投資基金(GPIF)基金(1.85兆美元全球
最大養老基金)進場購買日本公債。同時，美國財長
貝森特示警日圓空頭交易，表示｢掌握不對稱資訊，
現在莊家是我｣。加上日本公布7月名目現金薪資年增
4.7%，創29年最大升幅，日本央行貨幣政策正常化
路徑延續。貝森特支持日本以美國公債作為抵押，讓

日本在不出售美國公債的情況下買入日圓 (FIMA-外
國與國際貨幣當局附買回機制)。目前日本持有1.1兆
美元公債，同時加上美國政府表態支持干預弱勢日圓，
美日聯手讓日圓短線貶勢暫時告一段落。美元兌日圓
9月在160再度形成一波回檔，顯示美日政府力守160
整點關卡的決心。目前日本利率(無擔保隔夜拆款利
率)1.25%水準再同時加入日圓隔夜指數交換利率隱
含升息3碼，2027年利率水準與歐美差距仍有一段距
離，實質利率仍為負值，且在成熟市場中偏低。除此
之外，高市政府擴張性財政政策及新增支出計畫恐持
續不利日圓。高市於7月30日宣布自2027年4月起，
將現行8%食品消費稅率降至1%，實施期限為2年，

【市場短評】
受投資人低檔承接 ，美債殖利率從高檔回落 ，使金價從低
檔反彈 ，然受中東緊張情勢恐推升通膨 、消費者及生產者
物價數據高於預期 ，壓低金價 ，但美聯準會官員態度多認
為無升息急迫性 ，年底前才可能再升息的偏鷹言論 ，緩解
對10月升息預期 ，使美元及美債殖利率回落 ，支撐金價 ，
累計本週國際金價上漲 0.9%。下周關注美國製造業 PMI初
值、初請失業金人數及中東地緣緊張對油價的影響 ，預估
短期受偏高的債券殖利率及美元走強相對不利金價走勢 。
但若美債殖利率回落 、擔憂債務可持續性 、分散交易對手
風險及多元化資產配置 ，中國人行連續 23個月購金 ，並加
大購金步伐，預期中期金價仍有機會緩升。

## Document 559: 凱基投顧_SPCX SpaceX_張燾_20261021

Source PDF: all report pdf/凱基投顧_SPCX SpaceX_張燾_20261021.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-21
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

十月 21, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

動態更新  硬體  ‧美國

SpaceX 
(SPCX.OQ/SPCX US)

AI 算力租賃貢獻帶動營運由虧轉盈，並可望延續高成長性 
重要訊息 
SpaceX  3Q26 EPS 由虧轉盈並優於市場共識 ，主要來自 AI 業務轉盈；公司
給予 1Q27 營收較 3Q26 成長 140%之展望，盤後股價上漲約 7%。 
評論及分析  
3Q26 AI 營收跳升且轉盈，帶動 EPS 轉盈至 0.35 美元並優於共識。SpaceX 
3Q26 營收季增 86.5%至 145.8 億美元，動能來自 AI 業務營收季增 195%至
75.6 億美元，目前 AI 營收貢獻已超越衛星寬頻。在算力租賃契約陸續開始貢
獻下，3Q26 AI 業務之營業利益自 2Q26 虧損約 12.6 億美元轉盈至 4.3 億美
元，火箭業務亦隨公司增加 執行對外任務而使營業虧損收斂至 700 萬美元，
衛星寬頻業務亦隨用戶數增加而帶動營業利益季增 43%至 23.7 億美元，帶
動公司 EPS 轉盈至 0.35 美元，優於凱基與市場共識預估之 -0.12/0.24 美元。 
加速 AI 建置與 Starship 測試。4Q26 AI 算力租賃業務隨兩家不具名客戶分
別於 10 月與 12 月開始貢獻營收、單月分別貢獻約 11 億美元，預估帶動
4Q26 AI 業務營收季增 82%至 137 億美元。因應 AI 需求， SpaceX 規劃
4Q26 資本支出提升近 1 倍至 350-400 億美元，預計 4Q26 AI 建置量將自
3Q26 之 1.7GW 提升至 2.5-3GW，公司規劃算力約一半用於自用、一半租
賃，並提及新增算力預計在 1 年內回收投資成本。另外，第 15 次 Starship
試射預計於 11 月進行，此次將測試第一級推進器與第二級太空船回收能
力，並規劃 2027 年初測試第二節太空船復用能力，目前以 1H27 達到每週
發射 1 次 Starship、2H27 達到每週發射 2 次的頻率為目標。 
凱基上修 SpaceX  2026/2027 EPS 預估至-0.88/0.80 美元。基於 SpaceX AI
業務獲利改善狀況優於凱基原預期，且隨新客戶陸續貢獻，有助於 AI 營收
與獲利性逐步轉佳，故將 SpaceX 2026/2027 EPS 自原預估之- 1.64/ -0.57 美
元上修至- 0.88/0.80 美元。中長期而言，預期 SpaceX 主要獲利仍將來自衛
星寬頻與 AI 業務貢獻，凱基對長期財務預估大致不變。  
投資建議  
維持「增加持股」評等 ，目標價 227 美元(20 X FY2032  EPS 11.34 美元)。 
投資風險  
內部人持股陸續釋出， SpaceX 在外流通股數增加或為股價短期上行壓力。

股價說明 等級 定義 
 增加持股  (OP) 對個股持正面看法，預期個股未來十二個月的表現超越凱基證券集團所追蹤的相關市場的總報
酬。 
 持有 (N) 對個股持中性看法，預期個股未來十二個月的表現符合凱基證券集團所追蹤的相關市場的總報
酬。 
 降低持股 (U) 對個股持負面看法，預期個股未來十二個月的表現低於凱基證券集團所追蹤的相關市場的總報
酬。 
 未評等 (NR) 凱基證券未對該個股加以評等。  
 受法規限制 
未評等 (R) 
受凱基證券集團內部政策和 /或相關法令限制使凱基證券集團無法進行某些形式的資訊交流，其
中包括提供評等給投資人參考。  
  *總報酬 = (十二個月目標價 -現價)/現價

## Document 567: 凱基投顧_金融產業 施志鴻_20261015

Source PDF: all report pdf/凱基投顧_金融產業 施志鴻_20261015.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

十月 15, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 1

產業報告  台灣

金融產業

9 月銀行金控 營運及獲利 持穩，相較壽險、證券 波動加劇 
重要訊息  
13 家金控 9 月合併稅後淨利 529.7 億元， 月減及 年減各 39%及 19%，其中
銀行為主金控獲利 月減 11%，優於 壽險及證券獲利月減 57%及 27%，主要
銀行淨利息收入月增及呆帳提存下降 ，相較 9 月美債長天期殖利率 上揚 50 基
點影響壽險 FVTPL 債券評價及認列現金股利 收入減少，證券金控經紀手收 則
受台股日均量 連續三月量縮影響 。 
評論及分析  
壽險為主金控 ： FVTPL 投資收益 減少。國泰金 9 月稅後淨利 月減 41%至 67.2
億元，加計 FVOCI 股票資本利得 95.8 億元之調整後獲利月 減 20%至 163 億
元，主要國壽 9 月稅後淨利月減 74%至 17.6 億元(FVTPL 債券評價虧損 )，加
上證券月 減 25%(經紀手收減少 )，抵銷 銀行獲利月 增 12%(呆帳提存減少 )及
產險獲利月增 25%。國泰金 3Q26 稅後淨利 288.4 億元，季減 36%、年減
1%，帶動 前 9 月稅後淨利 年增 41%至 1,053 億元，調整後獲利年增 199%
至 2,237 億元，達凱基全年預估 80%及 85%，略優於預期。國壽 9 月淨值
比仍高於 12%，但淨值較上月略微下滑 。 
富邦金 9 月稅後淨利 月減 72%至 69.6 億元，加計實 現 FVOCI 股票資本利得
93.3 億元之調整後獲利月 減 57%至 162.9 億元，其中富壽 9 月稅後淨利 6.7
億元(FVTPL 股票及基金 部位虧損 2 億元， 債券評價 虧損 30 億元，抵銷 CSM
攤銷增加 )，加上銀行獲利月減 19%，抵銷證券獲利月增 3%。富邦金 3Q26
稅後淨利 299 億元，季減 53%、年減 25%，前 9 月金控獲利為 1,272 億元，
年增幅度減緩至 40%，但調整後獲利年增 175%至 2,500 億元，分別達凱基
全年預估 78%及 85%。此外， 富壽 9 月淨值比 維持超過 16%，意謂淨值仍
超過 9,000 億元。 
台新新光金 9 月稅後淨利 僅 44.2 億元，月減 37%、年增 8%，為今年單月新
低 ， 其中台新銀及新光銀獲利各月減 24%及 26%， 加上新壽獲利月減 10%。
台新新 光金 3Q26 稅後淨利 203.3 億元，季減 11%、年增 64%，略優於凱基
預估 1%，前 9 月金控 稅後淨利 年增 183% 至 642 億元 ，每股盈餘則年增 64%
至 2.47 元，達凱基全年預估之 79%。 
銀行為主金控 ：淨利息收入回升 。中信金 9 月稅後淨利 93.6 億元 ， 月減6%、
年減 16%，加計金控 FVOCI 股票資本利得 176 億元之調整後獲利月增 3.6%
至 269.6 億元，其中中信銀獲利月增 0.3%( 淨利差上揚提升淨利息收入、呆
帳提存僅 0.2 億元) ，加上創投認列獲利 14 億元反映投資部位評價回升，抵
銷台壽保獲利月減 58%至 12.9 億元。中信金 3Q26 稅後淨利 237 億元，季
增 44%、年減 5%，加上實現 FVOCI 股票資本利得合計 362 億元，3Q26 調
整後獲利 599 億元，季增 40%、年增 142%。中信金 前 9 月獲利年增 4%至
632 億元(每股盈餘 3.18 元)，調整後獲利年增 116% 至 1,315 億元(每股盈餘
6.68 元)，達凱基全年預估 76%及 104% ，優於預期 。

焦點內容

減各 39%、19%，其中銀行為主金控

月減 57%及 27%

回升，壽險 FVTPL 投資收益減少，相較
證券金控 經紀手收下滑影 響 
3. 4Q26F 金融股正向看法 ， 持續看好富邦
金 、 國泰金及中信金之 2026F 調整後獲

兆豐金 9 月稅後淨利 36.3 億元，月增 2%、年增 29%，主要證券及產險獲利
各月增 62%、39% ，抵銷銀行獲利月 減 13%(FX swap 收益及債券評價虧損
造成投資收益下滑 ，雖核心獲利淨利息收入及手續費收入仍較上月成長 )。
3Q26 兆豐金 獲利季減 3%、年增 14%，帶動前 9 月獲利至 328.5 億元，年
增率上揚至 16%，達凱基全年預估 78%。 
永豐金 9 月稅後淨利 僅 34.3 億元，月減 22%、年增 39%，其中永豐銀及證
券獲利各月增 0.1% 、0.3% ，抵銷京城銀獲利月減 42.5% (呆帳提存增加 )。
3Q26 永豐金 稅後淨利 122.3 億元，季減 10%、年增 55%，帶動前 9 月金控
稅後淨利年增幅度減緩至 79%，達凱基全年獲利預估 82%。 
玉山金 9 月稅後淨利 38 億元，月增 6%、年增 31%，其中銀行獲利月增 3%
抵銷證券獲利月減 12%，並首度認列三商壽獲利 1.44 億元，其與三商壽個
體財報之單月稅 後淨利 19.11 億元差異，主 依據 IFRS 3 反映合併基準日之利
率水準評價資產負債公允價值差異所致 。雖玉山金 自 9 月認列三商壽獲利，
但凱基預期三商壽獲利短期仍無法上繳至金控， 2026F 玉山金配息仍以銀行
及證券為主。  
第一金 9 月稅後淨利 23.8 億元，月 減 18.5%但年增 16%，主要 一銀獲利月
減 14%(FX swap 收益月減 6.2 至 4.5 億元，抵銷呆帳提存月減 9.8 億元)及壽
險小幅虧損 0.2 億元，抵銷證券獲利月增 13%。3Q26 第一金 獲利年增 9%、
季減 12%，前 9 月金控稅後淨利年增 21%至 262.3 億元，達凱基全年 預估
80%。 
元大金 9 月稅後淨利 50.2 億元，月 減 28%、年增 47%，主要證券獲利 月減
29%至 32.2 億元(投資收益及經紀手收各月減 6%及 28%( 台股日均量月 減
5.4%至 1.1 兆元之 4 月以來新低 )，加上銀行獲利月減 8%及壽險虧損 0.5 億
元。元大金 3Q26 獲利季減 17%、年增 76%，前 9 月稅後淨利 年增幅度減緩
至 106%。  
投資建議  
由於美國 9 月通膨未見改善 ， 市場預期美國聯準會 12 月再度升息 1 碼機率近
9 成，導致美國債券殖利率曲線上移，加劇資本市場波動 ，影響壽險、銀行及
證券之 FVTPL 投資部位 評價。此外，10 月以來台灣 10 年期公債殖利率亦突
破 2%大關 ， 反映出台灣9 月通膨增溫的趨勢，有助壽險台幣保單合約之負債
公允價值下降 ，若 4Q26F 通膨維持高檔， 市場預期央行可能於 12 月升息半
碼，有助銀行利差擴大 。目前凱基對 4Q26F 金融股維持正向看法， 持續看好
富邦金、國泰金及中信金 (實現資本利得 持續有助 2026F 調整後獲利、配息能
力及淨值上揚、銀行核心獲利成長)，兆豐金、上海商銀及永豐金(銀行核心獲
利成長及資產品質改善、 2026F 配息水準高於同業 )。 
投資風險  
美國聯準會升息 幅度高於市場預期， 資本市場波動 過大 影響金融業投資收
益；銀行信貸成本上揚高於預期。

股價說明 等級 定義 
 增加持股 (OP) 對個股持正面看法，預期個股未來十 二個月的表現超越凱基證券集團所追蹤的相關市場的總報
酬。 
 持有 (N) 對個股持中性看法，預期個股未來十二個月的表現符合凱基證券集團所追蹤的相關市場的總報
酬。 
 降低持股  (U) 對個股持負面看法，預期個股未來十二個月的表現低於凱基證券集團所追蹤的相關市場的總報
酬。 
 未評等 (NR) 凱基證券未對該個股加以評等。  
 受法規限制  
未評等 (R) 
受凱基證券集團內部政策和 /或相關法令限制使凱基證券集團無法進行某些形式的資訊交流，其
中包括提供評等給投資人參考。  
  *總報酬 = (十二個月目標價 -現價)/現價

## Document 591: 固本收利：聯準會升息了，投等債還能買嗎？

Source PDF: all report pdf/固本收利：聯準會升息了，投等債還能買嗎？.pdf

2026

 www.fubon.com

聯準會升息了，投等債還能買嗎？ 
 9 月聯準會升息一碼，為 2023 年 7 月以來首度升息，雖年內尚具升息空間，但
資金流仍持續流入美國投等債，顯示市況紛擾無損其資金避風港的特質。

出刊日：2026/10/8 
222 期

### Page 2

2026

02-8751-6665 
 www.fubon.com 
台北市大安區仁愛路四段 169 號 
固本收利

本期內容： 
⚫ 經濟數據、市場供
需等持續干擾債市 
⚫ 美國投等債仍為資
金良好避風港 
⚫ 建議逢利率回彈時
分批佈局投等債

重點摘要

■ 為因應頑固的通膨壓力 ，9 月聯準會升
息 1 碼，預期年內至少再升息 1 碼。
第 3 季美國投等債價格承壓是受美國
數據強勁、大量投等債發債、通膨預期
加上聯準會鷹派訊號等因素影響。

■ 總體經濟強韌提供絕佳的信用支撐，
高評級公司違約風險或信用降評機率
微乎其微。今年以來美國投資等級債
維持淨流入， 顯示市場動盪時美國投
等債仍為資金避風港。

■ 目前美國投等債殖利率 處於近年相對
較高的位置，投資人應以息收為主要
考量，建議投資人把握殖利率回彈時
機，分批進行佈局鎖定高債息收益。

▋ 聯準會 3 年多來首度升息 
9 月聯準會票委全數贊成通過升息 1 碼，將
聯邦資金利率目標調升為 3.75%至 4.00%
區間，為 2023 年 7 月以來首度升息。美國
經濟維持穩健擴張 ，聯準會對於 2026、
2027 年經濟成長預期均上修 0.1 個百分點
至 2.3%、2.4%，就業供需平穩且失業率穩
定，2026、2027 年失業率預期均下修 0.2
個百分點至 4.1%，會後聲明新增國內支出
具韌性描述。今年 PCE 和核心 PCE 均上修
0.1 個百分點至 3.7%和 3.4%，9 月聯準會
升息是為因應頑固的通膨壓力，18 名與會
官員中有 16 位委員預期今年內至少再升息
1 碼，明年聯準會維持 6 月的通膨預期， 政
策利率亦維持不變。(圖二、圖三)

圖二：美國經濟穩健，通膨具僵固性

▋ 殖利率飆壓抑 Q3 投等債價格 
第 3 季美國投等債價格下跌，是今年最差
單季表現 ， 相比去年第3 季以及過去 30 年
第 3 季的投等債價格表現亦相對遜色。這
是受到近期美國就業 、 生產數據表現強勁 ，
今年前 8 個月科技相關投等債發債量已超
越去年全年表現 ， 中東戰爭持續支撐國際油
價在高位 ， 而居高不下的通膨預期持續推升
美債殖利率走勢等 ， 加上聯準會主席Warsh
自 6 月主持個人首場利率會議起，即反覆
強調聯準會穩定物價的鷹派訊號， 持續為投
等債走勢構成壓力。(圖四、圖五)

▋ 那投資等級債還能買嗎？ 
美國經濟數據強勁意味在強韌的總體經濟
下 ， 提供絕佳的信用支撐 ， 即便面臨高利率
環境 ， 大型龍頭企業營運有望維持穩健 ， 加
上企業帳上現金充裕 ， 投資等級高的公司違
約風險或信用降評的機率將微乎其微。

隨著各國債券殖利率攀升 ，加劇全球風險資
產高檔震盪 ， 市場避險情緒明顯升溫， 今年
以來美國投資等級債維持淨流入， 根據小摩
數據顯示，至 9/24 流入投等債資金已直逼
2025 年，相比非投等債和新興市場債僅達
去年約一半的水準，顯示在市場動盪時 ， 美
國投資等級債的高流動性和 極佳避險韌性
仍為資金提供避風港而持續吸金。(圖六)

圖六：目前流入投等債資金直逼去年水準 
資料來源：JPM，2026/9/24

▋ 殖利率達近年高點具吸引力 
目前美國投等債利差落在近年低點 ， 相對較
無收斂空間 ， 但殖利率則落在5%至 6%間，
處於近年相對較高的位置 ， 高殖利具有投資
吸引力 ， 投資人應以息收為主要考量 ， 目前
包含金融公司、通訊產業債殖利率在6%以
上 ， 為殖利率相對領先的產業族群 ， 建議在
當前升息預期濃厚下 ， 投資人應把握殖利率
回彈的時機 ， 分批進行佈局以鎖定高債息收
益。(圖七、圖八)

圖七：目前投等債以息收為主 
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/9/24

圖八：目前金融 、 科技等投等債殖利率較高 
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/9/24

## Document 603: 多家公司，三季度净利环比增超50%

Source PDF: all report pdf/多家公司，三季度净利环比增超50%.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

10 ⽉ 22 ⽇晚间多家公司披露三季报，据不完全统计，当⽇有 10 家公司第三季度归⺟净利润
环⽐增超 50% ，包括⾏云科技、奥⼠康等。这些单季利润陡增的公司集中指向两条线索：
PCB 产业链上游基材的量价⻬升，以及算⼒基础设施需求的规模化兑现。
摘要：
10 ⽉ 22 ⽇晚间，沪深两市上市公司继续集中披露 2026 年三季度报告。据不完全统计，当⽇有 10
家公司第三季度归⺟净利润环⽐增超 50% ，其中⾏云科技环⽐增幅约 655% ，奥⼠康约 470% ，
中富电路约 303% ，华正新材约 341% ，晶丰明源约 230% ，华盛昌约 179% ，同花顺约 55% ，凯
莱英约 53% ，宏和科技约 52% ，⼭东玻纤约 52% 。
这些单季利润陡增的公司集中指向两条线索： PCB 产业链上游基材的量价⻬升，以及算⼒基础
设施需求的规模化兑现。
PCB 产业链量价⻬升，基材环节最为受益
PCB 产业链是当晚环⽐⾼增公司最集中的板块，包括华正新材、宏和科技、中富电路、奥⼠康
等 4 家公司。

从各公司公告的业绩变动原因看，上游基材环节的表述⾼度⼀致。华正新材在三季报中表⽰，
业绩增⻓主要系覆铜板单价及销量上升，带动营业收⼊及⽑利率增加；公司第三季度实现归⺟
净利润 6.27 亿元，同⽐增⻓ 3044.60% ，环⽐增⻓ 341% ；前三季度归⺟净利润 7.99 亿元，同⽐
增⻓ 1177.71% 。
宏和科技将增⻓归因于电⼦布销售单价上涨、特种电⼦布销量占⽐增加，公司第三季度归⺟净
利润 3.64 亿元，同⽐增⻓ 607.73% ；前三季度同⽐增⻓ 435.63% 。
中富电路在三季报中将增⻓归因于订单增⻓、⽣产规模扩⼤。奥⼠康第三季度归⺟净利润 3.71 亿
元，环⽐增⻓ 470% ，其前三季度归⺟净利润为 4.54 亿元。
芯⽚设计企业晶丰明源的情况略有特殊，公司在三季报中说明，剔除四川易冲后营业收⼊同⽐
增⻓明显，叠加并购四川易冲影响，第三季度归⺟净利润 1.65 亿元，环⽐增⻓ 230%—— 并购并
表与⾃⾝景⽓回暖在其单季⾼增中同时起作⽤。
重整公司业务重构，算⼒收⼊单季兑现

环⽐增幅最⼤的⾏云科技，其业绩变化背后是业务的整体重构。公司三季报显⽰， 2026 年是⾏
云科技完成破产重整、产业投资⼈正式接管公司后的⾸个完整会计年度，业务模式较上年发⽣
重⼤变化；公司确⽴跨境数字贸易与 AI 算⼒基础设施双主业发展战略，服务器贸易业务⾃ 2026
年 8 ⽉起实现规模化交付。
第三季度，公司实现营业收⼊ 9.21 亿元，上年同期为 0.14 亿元；前三季度营业收⼊由上年同期的
0.59 亿元增⾄ 11.75 亿元。算⼒侧的订单储备已在公告中可⻅：公司近期公告，经协商与 VB 客⼾
重新签订算⼒服务协议及补充协议，协议总⾦额上涨⾄ 33.57 亿元；此前的半年报披露，公司累
计落地超 154 亿元⻓期算⼒、存储框架合同；据投资者关系活动记录，公司今年已公告披露算⼒
及存储相关订单合计规模约 183.16 亿元。需要提⽰的是，公司在三季报中⾃述业务模式发⽣重
⼤变化，其同⽐、环⽐数据的可⽐性需结合重整背景甄别。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

⻛险提⽰及免责条款

中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

图⽚
其余公司的单季改善各有来路。同花顺在三季报中将增⻓归因于增值电信业务及⼴告与互联⽹
推⼴业务等收⼊增加，公司第三季度归⺟净利润 10.85 亿元，环⽐增⻓ 55% 。凯莱英第三季度归
⺟净利润 3.32 亿元，环⽐增⻓约 53% 。公司在三季报中预计第四季度交付规模较第三季度将⼤
幅增加、 2026 年全年营业收⼊增⻓ 19% ⾄ 22% 。⼭东玻纤的三季报将增⻓归因于玻纤纱价格同
⽐上涨、销量同⽐增加，公司前三季度归⺟净利润 5839.72 万元，同⽐扭亏为盈。
除此之外，华盛昌在三季报中未披露具体的业绩变动原因。公司第三季度归⺟净利润 1.60 亿元、
环⽐增⻓ 179% 。
本⽂来源：上海证券报

### Page 4

日本
(株)日本総合研究所 日本経済展望 2026年10月3
企業の景況感は総じて良好
◆製造業の景況感は改善
日銀短観９月調査によると 、大企業・製造業の
業況判断ＤＩは前回調査比＋２ ％ポイント上昇。
ＡＩ・半導体関連の需要拡大を背景に 、機械工
業が好調なほか、非鉄金属など関連業種の景況感
も改善。大企業・非製造業は前回調査比 ▲２％
ポイント低下。燃料費や人件費などのコスト増加が
重石となり 、小売や宿泊 ・飲食サービスなどで景況
感が悪化。
◆熊本地震が生産を下押し
製造業の生産活動は持ち直し。８月の鉱工業生
産は、前月比▲1.7％と減少。熊本地震による工
場の操業停止を受けた自動車工業を中心に幅広
い業種で減産。ただし、堅調なＡＩ関連需要を背
景に、生産用機械工業が増加するなど 、生産活動
は基調として持ち直し。
先行きの生産計画によると 、９月の製造工業生
産予測指数（過去の予測誤差を基に経済産業省
が算出した補正値）は同＋1.6％と上昇する見込
み。好調な半導体需要を背景に、機械工業が増産

する見通し。
◆中国人の渡航自粛が訪日需要の重石に
８月の訪日外客数は前年同月比▲9.6%と減少。
中国政府による日本への渡航自粛の呼びかけの影
響が続き、中国人訪日客の減少が全体を押し下げ。
もっとも、中国以外の主要国・地域からの訪日外客
数はコロナ禍前を上回る水準に回復。

日本
(株)日本総合研究所 日本経済展望 2026年10月4
財輸出は資本財を中心に持ち直し
◆財輸出は資本財が好調
財輸出は振れを伴いながらも持ち直し 。財別にみ
ると、資本財が増加し、2022年以来の高水準。世
界的なＡＩブームを背景に、とりわけ半導体等製造
装置の輸出が好調。
先行きの財輸出は 、資本財を中心に持ち直しの
動きが続く見通し。海外からの機械受注額は大きく
増加しており、今後の輸出を押し上げる公算大。半
導体市況の好調などを背景に 、韓国や台湾などの
半導体メーカーが工場を新設する予定 。これらのグ
ローバル企業による設備投資の拡大は、わが国が強
みを持つ半導体等製造装置輸出の追い風となる見
込み。
◆企業の投資意欲には底堅さ
設備投資は総じて持ち直し 。総固定資本形成を
形態別にみると、研究・開発サービスの一時的要因
などにより知的財産生産物投資が落ち込んだものの、
建設投資は横ばい圏を維持したほか 、機械投資は
増加。
先行きの設備投資は持ち直す見通し 。企業の設
備投資意欲は底堅さを維持。９月の日銀短観によ

れば、2026年度の設備投資計画は 、ソフトウェア
投資で一部弱さが見られたものの 、全体として昨年
並みの高水準。前回調査で落ち込みが懸念された
中小企業を含め 、設備不足感や人手不足感を背
景に、生産能力増強や省力化に向けた投資需要
が根強い状況。

### Page 6

日本
(株)日本総合研究所 日本経済展望 2026年10月5
良好な所得環境が個人消費を下支え
◆実質賃金は上昇
８月の一般労働者の所定内給与は前年比＋
3.8％と高い伸びを維持 。パートタイム労働者 （時
給ベース）も同＋4.1％と、高い伸び。こうした賃金
の増勢を受けて、実質賃金は同＋1.5％と、８ヵ月
連続でプラス圏で推移。
先行きも、実質賃金は前年比プラスでの推移が続
く見通し。人手不足感の高まりを背景に企業は賃
上げ姿勢を強めているほか、最低賃金の引き上げも
パートタイム労働者を中心に賃金を押し上げ 。今年
度の最低賃金の引き上げ率は前年比＋ 5.0％と、
前年度から幾分鈍化したものの 、例年対比で高い
水準。
◆消費は先行き底堅く推移
８月の消費活動指数は、サービス消費が引き続き
堅調に推移 。一方、食品価格の高止まりなどを背
景に非耐久財が伸び悩んでいるほか、耐久財でもエ
アコンの駆け込み需要や自動車の減税に伴う需要
増加による押し上げがはく落。
先行き、個人消費は緩やかに回復する見込み 。

実質賃金の上昇に加えて、消費者マインドが足元で
改善していることも下支え要因に。ただし、資源価格
の高騰により物価上昇圧力が強まった場合 、実質
賃金の下振れを通じて消費が腰折れする可能性に
注意。
代わりに最低賃金の図表を
ここにお願いします

継続されると想定
◆政策要因を除いた物価の伸びは高止まり
８月のコアＣＰＩ（生鮮食品を除く総合）は前
年比＋1.7％と、２％割れ。半導体市況の高騰な
どを背景にスマートフォンなどコア財の伸びが加速した
一方、政府の物価高対策がエネルギー価格を抑制 。
こうした政策などの特殊要因を除いた物価の伸び
（日銀公表値）は同＋2.6％と、２％を上回って
おり、物価上昇圧力は根強い状況。
先行きの物価は伸びが加速する見通し 。中東危
機の再燃を受けて原油価格が高止まりしているほか 、
冬場にかけて家計向け電気 ・ガス代抑制策の終了

が予定されており 、エネルギー価格の押し上げ要因
に。また、賃金上昇を起点とする基調的なインフレ圧
力が強まる見込み 。足元では、生産性の伸びを上
回るペースで賃金が上昇しており、企業の価格転嫁
を通じて販売価格の押し上げにつながりやすい状況 。
◆日銀は利上げペースを加速へ
日銀は、９月の金融政策決定会合で 、政策金
利を1.25％程度に引き上げ 。基調的なインフレ率
が２％に近付いているとして 、利上げを継続する構
え。次回利上げは2027年１月と予想。その後は四
半期に１回程度のペースで利上げし 、27年半ばに
政策金利は２％に達する見通し。
長期金利は 、海外金利の影響を受けながら 、日
銀の利上げ観測や財政支出の拡大観測などを背
景に、緩やかな上昇傾向をたどる見通し。エネルギー
補助金や消費減税を巡り 、財政規律の低下が強く
意識されれば、長期金利が上振れするリスクも。

### Page 8

日本
(株)日本総合研究所 日本経済展望 2026年10月7
トピックス①：金利上昇が住宅市場に及ぼす影響は変容
◆変わるハウジング・チャネルの波及経路
住宅ローン金利は上昇。金融政策が住宅市場を
通じて経済に波及する経路であるハウジング ・チャネ
ルは、近年、住宅価格を通じた波及が弱まる一方、
債務返済負担を通じた波及が強まっている可能性 。
その要因として、以下の２点が指摘可能。
第１に、変動金利型住宅ローンの普及により 、利
上げの影響は新規債務者だけでなく既存の債務者
にも波及しやすい傾向に。返済額の急増を抑える仕
組みにより影響は時間をかけて現れるものの、仮に、
政策金利が２％程度まで上昇した際、中長期的な
返済負担が相応に増え 、39歳以下の世帯では年
間返済負担の増加額は平均40万円近くに。
第２に、東京などの大都市では 、利上げによる住
宅価格の下押し効果が弱まっている可能性 。住宅
供給の制約があるほか 、人口や企業の集積を背景
に将来期待が強いうえ 、高所得層による現金購入

の増加など、金利変動の影響を受けにくい需要も拡
大。この結果、金利上昇による需要抑制効果が一
部弱められ、住宅価格の調整が進みにくい状況に。
◆資産形成などを通じた格差拡大も
このようなハウジング・チャネルの変化は、若年層や
子育て世帯の負担を高める可能性。既存債務者へ
の影響は、低金利時代に多額の借り入れを行った
20～30代世帯で大。都市部で住宅価格が十分に
調整しない場合、新たに住宅取得を目指す世帯で
は高い住宅価格と金利上昇の双方が重荷となり 、
住宅取得が困難に 。この結果、住宅保有を通じた
資産形成の開始が遅れ、世代間や資産保有状況
による格差の拡大につながる恐れも。

販売価格判断DI 仕入価格判断DI
（「上昇」－「下落」、％ポイント）
（資料）日本銀行を基に日本総研作成 （年/期）
食料品製造業の価格判断DI
◆日照不足で相次ぐ野菜の生育不順
今夏は東日本を中心に記録的な長雨が発生 。と
りわけ東京では 、観測史上最長となる８月末から
10月初まで37日連続の雨天に。全国的にみても、
９月の日照時間は記録的な低水準で推移。
こうした長雨による日照不足が、野菜の生育不順
を通じて生鮮食品価格を押し上げる恐れ 。農林水
産省が主産地や卸売会社などを対象に実施した聞
き取り調査によれば、東日本の主産地を中心に多く
の野菜で生育不順が指摘されている状況 。一部の

野菜では既に価格高騰の兆しがみられており、レタス
や白菜などの葉物野菜の卸売価格は足元で平年
の２倍近い水準に。大根やほうれん草といった冬野
菜でも生育不順がみられていることから 、冬場以降
にかけても価格が高止まりする可能性。
◆懸念される消費の腰折れ
こうした生鮮食品の価格上昇の影響は 、加工食
品や外食の値上げにも波及する可能性 。食品メー
カーや飲食店などでは 、近年、仕入れコストの増加
分を販売価格に転嫁する動きを強めており、原材料
価格の高騰が消費者物価に影響しやすい構造に。
食料品価格の上昇が広範に及べば、消費の先行
き懸念を強める要因に 。食料品など購入頻度が高
い生活必需品の価格上昇は 、家計の負担感を強
めやすく、消費者マインドの下押しにつながりやすい
傾向。消費者心理の悪化が 、足元で回復途上の
個人消費を一段と下押しする可能性に注意。

海外経済の前提 過去の実質ＧＤＰ予測値
先行きの景気は緩やかに回復するも、下振れリスクあり
9
わが国主要経済指標の予測値（2026年10月14日時点）
（資料）内閣府、総務省、経済産業省、財務省などを基に日本総研作成
◆景気は緩やかな回復基調を維持
わが国景気は一部で足踏みがみられるものの 、先
行き緩やかな回復基調に復する見通し 。ＷＴＩ原
油先物価格は 、中東危機の再燃を受けて当面高
止まりするものの、次第に緩やかに下落していく想定。
企業部門では、設備投資が持ち直す見通し 。中
東危機の再燃を受けた原油価格の高止まりが重石
となるものの 、省人化などに向けた投資への需要が
根強いほか 、政府による投資支援策も追い風に 。
世界的なＡＩ関連需要の高まりも 、輸出を下支え
する見込み。
家計部門では、個人消費が底堅く推移する見通
し。原油高が物価上昇圧力となるものの 、政府によ
る家計支援策がその影響を一定程度緩和すると想
定。高率の賃上げが続くなか 、実質賃金はプラス圏
で推移し、良好な所得環境が続くと予想。2027年

度からは食料品にかかる消費税率が引き下げられる
ことも、消費の下支え要因に。
◆中東情勢の深刻化と金利上昇がリスク
2026年度の実質ＧＤＰ成長率は＋1.0％、27
年度は＋0.9％と予想。もっとも、中東情勢が想定
よりも長期化・深刻化した場合、供給制約の強まり
や輸出の下押し、物価上昇に伴う実質所得の減少
などの経路から、景気への下押し圧力が一段と強ま
るリスク。また、高市政権の財政スタンスを巡って市
場の財政懸念が強まれば、長期金利が想定以上に
上昇し、民間需要を下押しするほか 、国債費の増
加などを通じて、投資支援策やガソリン補助金 、消
費減税といった政策を実施・継続する余地が狭まる
可能性。

米中対立の常態化や米国の自国第一主義の強まりを背景に 、既存の国際経済秩序が揺らいでいます 。こうしたなか、各国は存
在感を高めるグローバルサウス諸国との外交 ・経済関係の強化を急いでいます。日本も、OECD（経済協力開発機構）などと協調し
ながら、関係強化を目指していますが、現状はASEANやインドなどに偏重しており、アフリカや中南米との連携は限定的です。本シン
ポジウムでは、はじめに世界経済の構造変化や日本のグローバルサウス諸国との連携策の現状・課題を多角的に検証します。その
上で、日本がグローバルサウス諸国との関係を深化させるための具体的な戦略を議論します。
